</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 0.25                   &= 1 \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {1} {0.25} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {100} {25} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {25 \times 4} {25} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 4} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
a                               &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

The missing number $= 4$

</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}

<div class='question question'>

My four pet monkeys collected a pile of $60$ peanuts.

Monkey $A$ woke in the night and ate half of them,

then Monkey $B$ woke and ate one third of what was left, 

then Monkey $C$ woke and ate one quarter of the rest 

and finally Monkey $D$ woke and ate one fifth of what remainder.

</div>

<div class='working placeholder'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many peanuts did Monkey $D$ eat?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey A}          &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {60} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                        &= 30 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey B}          &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times (60 - 30) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= 10 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey C}          &= \dfrac {1} {4} \times (60 - 30 - 10) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                                        &= 5 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey D}          &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times (60 - 30 - 10 - 5) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {15} {5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                        &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Alternate solution.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey D}          &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times \left(\dfrac {3} {4} \times \left(\dfrac {2} {3} \times \left(\dfrac {1} {2} \times 60 \right) \right) \right) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times \dfrac {3} {4} \times \dfrac {2} {3} \times \dfrac {1} {2} \times 60  \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {1 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1 \times 60} {5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 5 \times 4 \times 3} {5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
                                        &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey A}          &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {60} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                        &= 30 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey B}          &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times (60 - 30) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= 10 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey C}          &= \dfrac {1} {4} \times (60 - 30 - 10) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                                        &= 5 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey D}          &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times (60 - 30 - 10 - 5) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {15} {5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                        &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Alternate solution.

                                        &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times \dfrac {3} {4} \times \dfrac {2} {3} \times \dfrac {1} {2} \times 60  \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {1 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1 \times 60} {5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2} \\\\

<div class='uuid'>
<p>2E6BAFD0-6BF4-4F43-9ADB-5353D4C15BDC</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2:1:12</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

$40 \%$ of the children on a school trip are boys and there are $72$ girls. How many children are on the trip?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of children $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(100 - 40) \% \ \text{of} \ a         &= 72 \\\\
\dfrac {60} {100} \times a          &= 72 \\\\
a                                   &= 72 \times \dfrac {100} {60} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {72 \times 100} {60} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {12 \times 6 \times 100} {6 \times 10} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel 6 \times 10 \cancel 0} {\cancel 6 \times 1 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                   &= 12 \times 10 \\\\
a                                   &= 120
\end{aligned}
$

The total number of children $= 120$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of children $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(100 - 40) \% \ \text{of} \ a         &= 72 \\\\
\dfrac {60} {100} \times a          &= 72 \\\\
a                                   &= 72 \times \dfrac {100} {60} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {72 \times 100} {60} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {12 \times 6 \times 100} {6 \times 10} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel 6 \times 10 \cancel 0} {\cancel 6 \times 1 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                   &= 12 \times 10 \\\\
a                                   &= 120
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The total number of children $= 120$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$120$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

If Luke starts with $3.7$, what answer will he get?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3.7 \times 2 - 5 \\\\
&= 7.4 - 5 \\\\
&= 2.4
\end{aligned}
$

He will get $= 2.4$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2.4$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<emptyline>

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What number should Luke start with to get an answer of $24?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 - 5          &= 24 \\\\
2a                      &= 24 + 5 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {29} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {29 \times 5} {2 \times 5} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {145} {10} \\\\
a                       &= 14.5
\end{aligned}
$

The number he start with $= 14.5$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$

$

2a                      &= 24 + 5 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {29} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {29 \times 5} {2 \times 5} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {145} {10} \\\\
a                       &= 14.5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number he start with $= 14.5$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many people chose water?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of people who choose water}    &= 20 - \dfrac {1} {4} \times 20 - 7 - 30 \% \ \text{of} \ 20 \\\\
                                            &= 20 - \dfrac {20} {4} - 7 - \dfrac {30} {100} \times 20 \\\\
                                            &= 13 - \dfrac {4 \times 5} {4} - \dfrac {30 \times 20} {100} \\\\
                                            &= 13 - \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} - \dfrac {3 \cancel 0 \times 2 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                            &= 13 - 5 - 3 \times 2 \\\\
                                            &= 8 - 6 \\\\
                                            &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of people who choose water}    &= 20 - \dfrac {1} {4} \times 20 - 7 - 30 \% \ \text{of} \ 20 \\\\
                                            &= 20 - \dfrac {20} {4} - 7 - \dfrac {30} {100} \times 20 \\\\
                                            &= 13 - \dfrac {4 \times 5} {4} - \dfrac {30 \times 20} {100} \\\\
                                            &= 13 - \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} - \dfrac {3 \cancel 0 \times 2 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                            &= 13 - 5 - 3 \times 2 \\\\
                                            &= 8 - 6 \\\\
                                            &= 2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Maya drew a pie chart to show all their choices. How many degrees should 'Water' have on her pie chart?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Degrees for water on chart}           &= \dfrac {2} {20} \times 360 \ \text{degrees} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 360} {20} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 18 \times 20} {20} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 18 \times \cancel {20}} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
                                            &= 2 \times 18 \\\\
                                            &= 36 \ \text{degrees}
\end{aligned}
$

Water should have $36 \ \text{degrees}$ on the pie chart.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36 \ \text{degrees}$

</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The difference between $\dfrac{1}{3}$ of a certain number and $\dfrac{1}{4}$ of the same number is $3$. 

What is the number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {3} \times a - \dfrac {1} {4} \times a                       &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {3} - \dfrac {a} {4}                                         &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3}     &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {4a} {12} - \dfrac {3a} {12}                                     &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {4a - 3a} {12}                                                   &= 3 \\\\
4a - 3a                                                                 &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                       &= 36
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {3} \times a - \dfrac {1} {4} \times a                       &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {3} - \dfrac {a} {4}                                         &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3}     &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {4a} {12} - \dfrac {3a} {12}                                     &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {4a - 3a} {12}                                                   &= 3 \\\\
4a - 3a                                                                 &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                       &= 36
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$383$ and $6226$ are examples of palindromic numbers as they read the 
same when the order of their digits is reversed. 

Write down the largest five-digit palindromic number that is a multiple of $5$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Unit place digit of multiple of $5$ $= 0, 5$

As per the question,

Number is palindromic so,

Digit at unit place $= $ Digit at $10000$ th place.

The largest five-digit palindromic number that is a multiple of $5 = 59995$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$59995$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Unit place digit of multiple of $5$ $= 0, 5$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
Number is palindromic so,
<emptyline>
Digit at unit place $= $ Digit at $10000$ th place.
<emptyline>
The largest five-digit palindromic number that is a multiple of $5 = 59995$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$59995$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The order of the digits is reversed in a certain two-digit whole number. 

This gives a new whole number which is one less than half of the original number. What is the original number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the digit at ones place $= a$

Digit at tens place $= b$

Original number $= 10b + a$

Reversed number $= 10a + b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
10a + b                     &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (10b + a) - 1 \\\\
10a + b                     &= \dfrac {10b + a} {2} - 1 \\\\
10a + b + 1                 &= \dfrac {10b + a} {2} \\\\
2 \times (10a + b + 1)      &= 10b + a \\\\
20a + 2b + 2                &= 10b + a \\\\
2                           &= 10b - 2b + a - 20a \\\\
2                           &= 8b - 19a \\\\
8b - 19a                    &= 2 \\\\
8b                          &= 19a + 2 \\\\
b                           &= \dfrac {19a + 2} {8}
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out a number that satisfies the above equation.

|  a  |          19a + 2        | Multiple of 8 |
|:---:|:-----------------------:|:-------------:|
| $1$ |  $19 \times 1 + 2 = 21$ |      No       |
| $2$ |  $19 \times 2 + 2 = 40$ |      Yes      |

The digit at ones place $= 2$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The digit at tens place}          &= \dfrac {19 \times 2 + 2} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {40} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel 8} \\\\
                                        &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

The Original number $= 52$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$52$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the digit at ones place $= a$
<emptyline>
Digit at tens place $= b$
<emptyline>
Original number $= 10b + a$
<emptyline>
Reversed number $= 10a + b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
10a + b                     &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (10b + a) - 1 \\\\
10a + b                     &= \dfrac {10b + a} {2} - 1 \\\\
10a + b + 1                 &= \dfrac {10b + a} {2} \\\\
2 \times (10a + b + 1)      &= 10b + a \\\\
20a + 2b + 2                &= 10b + a \\\\
2                           &= 10b - 2b + a - 20a \\\\
2                           &= 8b - 19a \\\\
8b - 19a                    &= 2 \\\\
8b                          &= 19a + 2 \\\\
b                           &= \dfrac {19a + 2} {8}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out a number that satisfies the above equation.
<emptyline>
|  a  |          19a + 2        | Multiple of 8 |
|:---:|:-----------------------:|:-------------:|
| $1$ |  $19 \times 1 + 2 = 21$ |      No       |
| $2$ |  $19 \times 2 + 2 = 40$ |      Yes      |
<emptyline>
The digit at ones place $= 2$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The digit at tens place}          &= \dfrac {19 \times 2 + 2} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {40} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel 8} \\\\
                                        &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The Original number $= 52$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$52$
</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>58BAFA2D-B38E-4A53-8822-FF8913C2BE37</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2:2:5</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Hareni has twice as many sweets as Dora. 
After eating $18$ sweets each, she has five times as many as Dora. How many sweets did Hareni start with?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let at the start number of sweets Dora has $= a$

Number of sweets Hareni has $= 2a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times (a - 18)       &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 5 \times 18        &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 90                 &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 2a                 &= 90 - 18 \\\\
3a                      &= 72 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {72} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 24} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 24} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                       &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of sweets Hareni has}       &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
                                         &= 48
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let at the start number of sweets Dora has $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of sweets Hareni has $= 2a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times (a - 18)       &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 5 \times 18        &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 90                 &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 2a                 &= 90 - 18 \\\\
3a                      &= 72 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {72} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 24} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 24} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                       &= 24
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of sweets Hareni has}       &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
                                         &= 48
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$48$
</code></pre>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Three cereal bars, two apples and one tube of mints cost $\pounds 3.14$.

Two cereal bars, three apples and four tube of mints cost $\pounds 4.21$.

What is the total cost of buying one cereal bar, one apple and one tube of mints?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the price of one cereal bar $= c$ 

Price of one apple $= a$

Price of one tube of mint $= m$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3c + 2a + m             &= 3.14 \\\\
2c + 3a + 4m            &= 4.21
\end{aligned}
$

Let's add both the equations.

$
\begin{aligned}
3c + 2a + m + 2c + 3a + 4m          &= 3.14 + 4.21 \\\\
5c + 5a + 5m                        &= 7.35 \\\\
5 (c + a + m)                       &= \dfrac {735} {100} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {5 \times 147} {100 \times 5} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 147} {100 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {147} {100} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \pounds 1.47
\end{aligned}
$

Total cost of one cereal bar, one apple and one tube of mint $= \pounds 1.47$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.47$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the price of one cereal bar $= c$ 
<emptyline>
Price of one apple $= a$
<emptyline>
Price of one tube of mint $= m$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3c + 2a + m             &= 3.14 \\\\
2c + 3a + 4m            &= 4.21
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's add both the equations.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3c + 2a + m + 2c + 3a + 4m          &= 3.14 + 4.21 \\\\
5c + 5a + 5m                        &= 7.35 \\\\
5 (c + a + m)                       &= \dfrac {735} {100} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {5 \times 147} {100 \times 5} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 147} {100 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {147} {100} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \pounds 1.47
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total cost of one cereal bar, one apple and one tube of mint $= \pounds 1.47$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1.47$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Each letter stands for a different number.

The totals for each row and column are shown.

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-2-question-7-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-2-question-7-subquestion-2-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-2-question-7-subquestion-2-00.png")

What is the value of each letter?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the value of A $= a$

Value of B $= b$

Value of C $= c$

Value of D $= d$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
d + d + d + d           &= 26 \\\\
4d                      &= 26 \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {26} {4} \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {26 \times 25} {4 \times 25} \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {650} {100} \\\\
d                       &= 6.5 \\\\
c + c + c + d           &= 17 \\\\
3c + d                  &= 17 \\\\
3c + 6.5                &= 17 \\\\
3c                      &= 17 - 6.5 \\\\
c                       &= \dfrac {10.5} {3} \\\\
c                       &= 3.5 \\\\
b + b + b + c           &= 11 \\\\
3b + c                  &= 11 \\\\
3b                      &= 11 - c \\\\
3b                      &= 11 - 3.5 \\\\
b                       &= \dfrac {7.5} {3} \\\\
b                       &= 2.5 \\\\
a + b + c + d           &= 22 \\\\
a + 2.5 + 3.5 + 6.5     &= 22 \\\\
a + 12.5                &= 22 \\\\
a                       &= 22 - 12.5 \\\\
a                       &= 9.5
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Value of A}           &= 9.5 \\\\
\text{Value of B}           &= 2.5 \\\\
\text{Value of C}           &= 3.5 \\\\
\text{Value of D}           &= 6.5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text{Value of A} = 9.5$

$\text{Value of B} = 2.5$

$\text{Value of C} = 3.5$

$\text{Value of D} = 6.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the value of A $= a$
<emptyline>
Value of B $= b$
<emptyline>
Value of C $= c$
<emptyline>
Value of D $= d$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
d + d + d + d           &= 26 \\\\
4d                      &= 26 \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {26} {4} \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {26 \times 25} {4 \times 25} \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {650} {100} \\\\
d                       &= 6.5 \\\\
c + c + c + d           &= 17 \\\\
3c + d                  &= 17 \\\\
3c + 6.5                &= 17 \\\\
3c                      &= 17 - 6.5 \\\\
c                       &= \dfrac {10.5} {3} \\\\
c                       &= 3.5 \\\\
b + b + b + c           &= 11 \\\\
3b + c                  &= 11 \\\\
3b                      &= 11 - c \\\\
3b                      &= 11 - 3.5 \\\\
b                       &= \dfrac {7.5} {3} \\\\
b                       &= 2.5 \\\\
a + b + c + d           &= 22 \\\\
a + 2.5 + 3.5 + 6.5     &= 22 \\\\
a + 12.5                &= 22 \\\\
a                       &= 22 - 12.5 \\\\
a                       &= 9.5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Value of A}           &= 9.5 \\\\
\text{Value of B}           &= 2.5 \\\\
\text{Value of C}           &= 3.5 \\\\
\text{Value of D}           &= 6.5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The tables in the Dining Hall can each seat $8$ pupils. 

How many tables are needed to seat $180$ pupils?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total tables needed}                    &= \dfrac {\text{Total pupils}} {\text{Pupil per table}} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {180} {8} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {45 \times 4} {2 \times 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {45 \times \cancel 4} {2 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {45} {2} \\\\
                                              &= 22 \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
                                              &= 23
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$23 \ \text{tables}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total tables needed}                    &= \dfrac {\text{Total pupils}} {\text{Pupil per table}} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {180} {8} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {45 \times 4} {2 \times 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {45 \times \cancel 4} {2 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {45} {2} \\\\
                                              &= 22 \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
                                              &= 23
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$23 \ \text{tables}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Jake paid a $\pounds 50$ deposit for a bike, then monthly
payments of $\pounds 25$ each. The bike cost $\pounds 275$. 

How many monthly payments did Jake have to make to buy it?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of monthly payments $= m$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
50 + 25m                                      &= 275 \\\\
25m                                           &= 275 - 50 \\\\
25m                                           &= 225 \\\\
m                                             &= \dfrac {225} {25} \\\\
m                                             &= \dfrac {9 \times 25} {25} \\\\
m                                             &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel{25}} {\cancel{25}} \\\\
m                                             &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

The number of monthly payments $= 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of monthly payments $= m$

m                                             &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel{25}} {\cancel{25}} \\\\
m                                             &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number of monthly payments $= 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

There are $500$ pupils at Lily's school.

There are $30$ more girls than boys. 

How many girls are there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of girls $= g$

Number of boys $= g - 30$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of girls} + \text{Number of boys}       &= \text{Total pupils} \\\\
g + (g - 30)                                         &= 500 \\\\
2g                                                   &= 500 + 30 \\\\
2g                                                   &= 530 \\\\
g                                                    &= \dfrac {530} {2} \\\\
g                                                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 265} {2} \\\\
g                                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times 265} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
g                                                    &= 265
\end{aligned}
$

Number of girls at Lily's school $= 265$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$265 \ \text{girls}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of girls $= g$
<emptyline>
Number of boys $= g - 30$
<emptyline>

$

g                                                    &= \dfrac {530} {2} \\\\
g                                                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 265} {2} \\\\
g                                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times 265} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
g                                                    &= 265
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of girls at Lily's school $= 265$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$265 \ \text{girls}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The sum of five consecutive whole numbers is $100$. 

What is the smallest of these five numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the five consecutive whole numbers $= a - 2, a - 1, a , a + 1, a + 2$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a - 2 + a - 1 + a + a + 1 + a + 2   &= 100 \\\\
a + a + a + a + a - 2 + 2 - 1 + 1   &= 100 \\\\
5a                                  &= 100 \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {100} {5} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                   &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smallest whole number}        &= a - 2 \\\\
                                    &= 20 - 2 \\\\
                                    &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the five consecutive whole numbers $= a - 2, a - 1, a , a + 1, a + 2$

<emptyline>

a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                   &= 20
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

                                    &= 20 - 2 \\\\
                                    &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The sum of two numbers is $22$ and their difference is $4$. 

What are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the two numbers $= a, b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b       &= 22 \\\\
a - b       &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

Let's add both the equations.

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b + a - b           &= 22 + 4 \\\\
a + a + b - b           &= 22 + 4 \\\\
2a                      &= 26 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 13} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 13} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

Let's substitute the value of a in one of the initial equations to find b.

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b                   &= 22 \\\\
b                       &= 22 - a \\\\
b                       &= 22 - 13 \\\\
b                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

The two numbers $= 13, 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$13, 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the two numbers $= a, b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + b       &= 22 \\\\
a - b       &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's add both the equations.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + b + a - b           &= 22 + 4 \\\\
a + a + b - b           &= 22 + 4 \\\\
2a                      &= 26 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 13} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 13} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's substitute the value of a in one of the initial equations to find b.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + b                   &= 22 \\\\
b                       &= 22 - a \\\\
b                       &= 22 - 13 \\\\
b                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The two numbers $= 13, 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$13, 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-3:1:13</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='question question'>

At Ali's shop, a multipack of $8$ toilet rolls costs $\pounds 2.40$. 

At Mya's shop, the cost per toilet roll is twice as much. 

How much is a pack of $3$ toilet rolls at Mya's shop?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost per toilet roll at Ali's shop}       &= \dfrac {2.4} {8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 3} {\cancel 8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3} {10} \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 0.3
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost per toilet roll at Mya's shop}       &= 0.3 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 0.6
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 3 toilet rolls at Mya's shop}       &= 0.6 \times 3 \\\\
                                                  &= \pounds 1.8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost per toilet roll at Ali's shop}       &= \dfrac {2.4} {8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 3} {\cancel 8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3} {10} \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 0.3
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost per toilet roll at Mya's shop}       &= 0.3 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 0.6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 3 toilet rolls at Mya's shop}       &= 0.6 \times 3 \\\\
                                                  &= \pounds 1.8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1.8$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-3:2:3</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='question question'>

Paddy is leading in a race. 

He is $81 \ \text{metres}$ in front of Sam who is in last place. 

Martin is between Paddy and Sam. 

Martin is $40 \ \text{metres}$ from the finish line and is twice as far away from Sam as he is from Paddy. 

How far has Sam left to run?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let distance of Paddy from finish line $= a$

Distance of Sam from finish line $= a + 81$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 81 - 40             &= 2 \times (40 - a) \\\\
a + 41                  &= 80 - 2a \\\\
a + 2a                  &= 80 - 41 \\\\
3a                      &= 39 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {39} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 13} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 13} { \cancel 3} \\\\
a                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance of Sam from the finish line}     &= 13 + 81 \\\\
                                                &= 94 \ \text{metres}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$94 \ \text{metres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let distance of Paddy from finish line $= a$
<emptyline>
Distance of Sam from finish line $= a + 81$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 81 - 40             &= 2 \times (40 - a) \\\\
a + 41                  &= 80 - 2a \\\\
a + 2a                  &= 80 - 41 \\\\
3a                      &= 39 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {39} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 13} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 13} { \cancel 3} \\\\
a                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance of Sam from the finish line}     &= 13 + 81 \\\\
                                                &= 94 \ \text{metres}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$94 \ \text{metres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

I thought of a number, halved it and then subtracted $9$.

I got an answer of $12$.

What was my original number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the original number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {2} - 9              &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 12 + 9 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 21 \\\\
a                               &= 2 \times 21 \\\\
a                               &= 42 
\end{aligned}
$

The original number $= 42$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$42$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the original number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {2} - 9              &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 12 + 9 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 21 \\\\
a                               &= 2 \times 21 \\\\
a                               &= 42 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The original number $= 42$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Fawzan went shopping with $\pounds 100$ in his wallet.

He spent $10 \%$ of his money on a book and $20 \%$ of the rest on a DVD. How much money did he then have left?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money spent on book}              &= 10 \% \ \text{of} \ 100 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10} {100} \times 100 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 100} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel {100}} \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 10
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left after buying books}            &= 100 - 10 \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 90
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money spent on DVD}               &= 20 \% \ \text{of} \ 90 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20} {100} \times 90 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 90} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \cancel 0 \times 9 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                        &= 2 \times 9 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 18
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left with Fawzan after shopping}        &= 90 - 18 \\\\
                                                    &= \pounds 72
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Alternate solution.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left with Fawzan after shopping}            &= 80 \% \ \text{of} \ \left( 90 \% \ \text{of} \ \pounds 100 \right) \\\\
                                                        &= \dfrac {80} {100} \times \dfrac {90} {100} \times 100 \\\\
                                                        &= \dfrac {80 \times 90 \times 100} {100 \times 100} \\\\
                                                        &= \dfrac {8 \cancel 0 \times 9 \cancel 0 \times \cancel {100}} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
                                                        &= 8 \times 9 \\\\
                                                        &= 72
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 72$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 100} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel {100}} \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 10
\end{aligned}
$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Abdul is making some small snacks for a party.

He cuts $1.2 \ \text{kg}$ of cheese into a number of $30 \ \text{g}$ pieces.

How many pieces does he have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total weight of cheese}       &= 1.2 \ \text{kg} \\\\
                                    &= 1.2 \times 1000 \ \text{g} \\\\
                                    &= 1200 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of 30 g pieces}    &= \dfrac {1200} {30} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {30 \times 40} {30} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {30} \times 40} {\cancel {30}} \\\\
                                &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note

$

                                &= \dfrac {30 \times 40} {30} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {30} \times 40} {\cancel {30}} \\\\
                                &= 40
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The sum of three consecutive whole numbers is $60$.

What is the largest of the three numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the three consecutive whole numbers $= a - 1, a, a + 1$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a - 1 + a + a + 1                   &= 60 \\\\
a + a + a - 1 + 1                   &= 60 \\\\
3a                                  &= 60 \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {60} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                   &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Largest number}               &= 20 + 1 \\\\
                                    &= 21
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$21$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the three consecutive whole numbers $= a - 1, a, a + 1$

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

\begin{aligned}

                                    &= 21
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

&= 60 \\\\

&= 30 \times 2 \\\\
&= 20 \times 3 \\\\
&= 15 \times 4 \\\\
&= 12 \times 5
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Find the right katex symbol.*

Given that,

$
\begin{aligned}
\spadesuit + \spadesuit                 &= \spadesuit \\\\
\spadesuit + \spadesuit                 &= \spadesuit \\\\
\spadesuit + \spadesuit + \spadesuit    &= \spadesuit 
\end{aligned}
$

how many $\spadesuit$ are equal to $\spadesuit$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the value of triangle $= t$

Value of square $= s$

Value of circle $= c$

Value of diamond $= d$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2t              &= s \\\\
s + t           &= c \\\\
d               &= c + s + t \\\\
c               &= 2t + t \\\\
c               &= 3t \\\\
d               &= 3t + 2t + t \\\\
d               &= 6t
\end{aligned}
$

$6$ triangles are equal to one diamond.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the value of triangle $= t$
<emptyline>
Value of square $= s$
<emptyline>
Value of circle $= c$
<emptyline>
Value of diamond $= d$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2t              &= s \\\\
s + t           &= c \\\\
d               &= c + s + t \\\\
c               &= 2t + t \\\\
c               &= 3t \\\\
d               &= 3t + 2t + t \\\\
d               &= 6t
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$6$ triangles are equal to one diamond.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What number does $m$ stand for, if $m * 6 = 41?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
m * 6                   &= 41 \\\\
(5 \times m) + 6        &= 41 \\\\
5m                      &= 41 - 6 \\\\
m                       &= \dfrac {35} {5} \\\\
m                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 7} {5} \\\\
m                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 7} {\cancel 5} \\\\
m                       &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

(5 \times m) + 6        &= 41 \\\\
5m                      &= 41 - 6 \\\\
m                       &= \dfrac {35} {5} \\\\
m                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 7} {5} \\\\
m                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 7} {\cancel 5} \\\\
m                       &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $n * 4 = 8 * n$, what number does $n$ stand for?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
n * 4                   &= 8 * n \\\\
(5 \times n) + 4        &= (5 \times 8) + n \\\\
5n + 4                  &= 40 + n \\\\
5n - n                  &= 40 - 4 \\\\
4n                      &= 36 \\\\
n                       &= \dfrac {36} {4} \\\\
n                       &= \dfrac {4 \times 9} {4} \\\\
n                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 9} {\cancel 4} \\\\
n                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
n * 4                   &= 8 * n \\\\
(5 \times n) + 4        &= (5 \times 8) + n \\\\
5n + 4                  &= 40 + n \\\\
5n - n                  &= 40 - 4 \\\\
4n                      &= 36 \\\\
n                       &= \dfrac {36} {4} \\\\
n                       &= \dfrac {4 \times 9} {4} \\\\
n                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 9} {\cancel 4} \\\\
n                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

I thought of a number, halved it and then subtracted $9$.

I got an answer of $12$.

What was my original number ?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the original number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {2} - 9              &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 12 + 9 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 21 \\\\
a                               &= 2 \times 21 \\\\
a                               &= 42 
\end{aligned}
$

The original number $= 42$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$42$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the original number $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {2} - 9              &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 12 + 9 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 21 \\\\
a                               &= 2 \times 21 \\\\
a                               &= 42 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The original number $= 42$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$42$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Fawzan went shopping with $\pounds 100$ in his wallet.

He spent $10 \%$ of his money on a book and $20 \%$ of the rest on a DVD. How much money did he then have left?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money spent on book}              &= 10 \% \ \text{of} \ 100 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10} {100} \times 100 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 100} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel {100}} \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 10
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left after buying books}            &= 100 - 10 \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 90
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money spent on DVD}               &= 20 \% \ \text{of} \ 90 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20} {100} \times 90 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 90} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \cancel 0 \times 9 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                        &= 2 \times 9 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 18
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left with Fawzan after shooping}            &= 90 - 18 \\\\
                                                        &= \pounds 72
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Alternate solution.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left with Fawzan after shopping}        &= 80 \% \ \text{of} \ \left( 90 \% \ \text{of} \ \pounds 100 \right) \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {80} {100} \times \dfrac {90} {100} \times 100 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {80 \times 90 \times 100} {100 \times 100} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {8 \cancel 0 \times 9 \cancel 0 \times \cancel {100}} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
                                                    &= 8 \times 9 \\\\
                                                    &= \pounds 72
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 72$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                                        &= \dfrac {10} {100} \times 100 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 100} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel {100}} \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 10
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$

                                                &= \pounds 90
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

                                        &= \dfrac {20} {100} \times 90 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 90} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \cancel 0 \times 9 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\

                                        &= \pounds 18
\end{aligned}
$

                                                        &= \pounds 72
\end{aligned}

                                                    &= \dfrac {80 \times 90 \times 100} {100 \times 100} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {8 \cancel 0 \times 9 \cancel 0 \times \cancel {100}} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\

</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Abdul is making some small snacks for a party.

He cuts $1.2 \ \text{kg}$ of cheese into a number of $30 \ \text{g}$ pieces.

How many pieces does he have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total weight of cheese}       &= 1.2 \ \text{kg} \\\\
                                    &= 1.2 \times 1000 \ \text{g} \\\\
                                    &= 1200 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of 30 g pieces}         &= \dfrac {1200} {30} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {30 \times 40} {30} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {30} \times 40} {\cancel {30}} \\\\
                                &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total weight of cheese}       &= 1.2 \ \text{kg} \\\\
                                    &= 1.2 \times 1000 \ \text{g} \\\\
                                    &= 1200 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of 30 g pieces}         &= \dfrac {1200} {30} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {30 \times 40} {30} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {30} \times 40} {\cancel {30}} \\\\
                                &= 40
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$40$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The sum of three consecutive whole numbers is $60$.

What is the largest of the three numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the three consecutive whole numbers $= a - 1, a, a + 1$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a - 1 + a + a + 1                   &= 60 \\\\
a + a + a - 1 + 1                   &= 60 \\\\
3a                                  &= 60 \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {60} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                   &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Largest number}               &= 20 + 1 \\\\
                                    &= 21
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$21$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the three consecutive whole numbers $= a - 1, a, a + 1$
<emptyline>

a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                   &= 20
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Largest number}               &= 20 + 1 \\\\
                                    &= 21
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$21$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the smallest positive whole number that divides exactly by
$1, 2, 3, 4$ and $5?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Smallest positive whole number $= 60$

Let's verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 60 \\\\
&= 60 \times 1 \\\\
&= 30 \times 2 \\\\
&= 20 \times 3 \\\\
&= 15 \times 4 \\\\
&= 12 \times 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

*ABBR: TODO:: Find the right katex symbol.*


Given that,

$
\begin{aligned}
\spadesuit + \spadesuit                 &= \spadesuit \\\\
\spadesuit + \spadesuit                 &= \spadesuit \\\\
\spadesuit + \spadesuit + \spadesuit    &= \spadesuit 
\end{aligned}
$

how many $\spadesuit$ are equal to $\spadesuit$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the value of triangle $= t$

Value of square $= s$

Value of circle $= c$

Value of diamond $= d$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2t              &= s \\\\
s + t           &= c \\\\
d               &= c + s + t \\\\
c               &= 2t + t \\\\
c               &= 3t \\\\
d               &= 3t + 2t + t \\\\
d               &= 6t
\end{aligned}
$

$6$ triangles are equal to one diamond.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the value of triangle $= t$
<emptyline>
Value of square $= s$
<emptyline>
Value of circle $= c$
<emptyline>
Value of diamond $= d$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2t              &= s \\\\
s + t           &= c \\\\
d               &= c + s + t \\\\
c               &= 2t + t \\\\
c               &= 3t \\\\
d               &= 3t + 2t + t \\\\
d               &= 6t
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$6$ triangles are equal to one diamond.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Eleanor receives $\pounds 20$ pocket money each week.
This week, she spent $\pounds 4.88$ on sweets at the shop.
She then put a third of what was left in her piggy bank.

How much money does she still have to spend?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left with Eleanor}      &= 20 - (4.88 + \dfrac {1} {3} \times (20 - 4.88)) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + \dfrac {1} {3} \times 15.12) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + \dfrac {15.12} {3}) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + 5.04) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + 5.04) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - 9.92 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 10.08
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 10.08$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left with Eleanor}      &= 20 - (4.88 + \dfrac {1} {3} \times (20 - 4.88)) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + \dfrac {1} {3} \times 15.12) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + \dfrac {15.12} {3}) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + 5.04) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - (4.88 + 5.04) \\\\
                                    &= 20 - 9.92 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 10.08
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 10.08$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

As everyone knows, a normal cat has $18$ claws, $5$ on each 
front leg and $4$ on each back leg.

At Harry's Care Home for Distressed Cats there are 
$4$ three-legged cats, each one with a different leg missing.

How many claws do they have all together?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Total claws per cat $= 18$

As per the  question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total claws}          &= (18 - 5) + (18 - 5) + (18 - 4) + (18 - 4) \\\\
                            &= 13 + 13 + 14 + 14 \\\\
                            &= 26 + 28 \\\\
                            &= 54
\end{aligned}
$

Total claws of $4$ three-legged cats $= 54$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$54$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Total claws per cat $= 18$
<emptyline>
As per the  question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total claws}          &= (18 - 5) + (18 - 5) + (18 - 4) + (18 - 4) \\\\
                            &= 13 + 13 + 14 + 14 \\\\
                            &= 26 + 28 \\\\
                            &= 54
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total claws of $4$ three-legged cats $= 54$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$54$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many packets of each type of sweet does she need to buy?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of raspberry creams}       &= 30 \times 3 \\\\
                                        &= 90 \\\\
\text{Number of pineapple jellies}      &= 30 \times 1 \\\\
                                        &= 30 \\\\
\text{Number of lemon sours}            &= 30 \times 2 \\\\
                                        &= 60
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of raspberry creams packets}       &= \dfrac {90} {15} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {15 \times 6} {15} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {15} \times 6} {\cancel {15}} \\\\
                                                &= 6 \\\\
\text{Number of pineapple jellies packets}      &= \dfrac {30} {10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                                &= 3 \\\\
\text{Number of lemon sours packets}            &= \dfrac {60} {20} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {20 \times 3} {20} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 3} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
                                                &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Raspberry creams packets $= 6$

Pineapple jellies packets $= 3$

Lemon sours packets $= 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of raspberry creams}       &= 30 \times 3 \\\\
                                        &= 90 \\\\
\text{Number of pineapple jellies}      &= 30 \times 1 \\\\
                                        &= 30 \\\\
\text{Number of lemon sours}            &= 30 \times 2 \\\\
                                        &= 60
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

                                                &= \dfrac {15 \times 6} {15} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {15} \times 6} {\cancel {15}} \\\\
                                                &= 6 \\\\
\text{Number of pineapple jellies packets}      &= \dfrac {30} {10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                                &= 3 \\\\
\text{Number of lemon sours packets}            &= \dfrac {60} {20} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {20 \times 3} {20} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 3} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
                                                &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

How much will she spend in total, when buying the sweets?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of raspberry creams}         &= 1.65 \times 6 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 9.9 \\\\
\text{Cost of pineapple jellies}        &= 1.5 \times 3 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 4.5 \\\\
\text{Cost of lemon sours}              &= 1.6 \times 3 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 4.8 \\\\
\text{Total cost}                       &= 9.9 + 4.5 + 4.8 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 19.2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 19.2$

</div>
</div>

                                        &= \pounds 4.8 \\\\
\text{Total cost}                       &= 9.9 + 4.5 + 4.8 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 19.2
\end{aligned}

If she sells her boxes for $\pounds 1$ each, how much profit will she make?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Selling Price}            &= 1 \times 30 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 30 \\\\
\text{Cost Price}               &= \pounds 19.2 \\\\
\text{Profit}                   &= \text{Selling Price} - \text{Cost Price} \\\\
                                &= 30 - 19.2 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 10.8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='answers'>

                                &= 30 - 19.2 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 10.8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>blackheath-high-school--11-plus--maths--2007--sample-paper-1:1:9</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
