<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Fill the spaces to make all these fractions equal:

$\dfrac{}{3} \quad = 
\quad \dfrac{2}{6} \quad = 
\quad \dfrac{}{18} \quad = 
\quad \dfrac{7}{} \quad = 
\quad \dfrac{}{60}
$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing numerator $= a,b,c$

Let the missing denominator $= d$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {3} &= \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac {2 \times 3} {6} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac {6} {6} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac {\cancel {6}} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
a              &= 1
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {b} {18} &= \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {2 \times 18} {6} \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 6} {2 \times 3} \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {3} \times 6} {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
b               &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {7} {d}          &= \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
\dfrac {7 \times 6} {2} &= d \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {7 \times 6} {2} \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {7 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
d                       &= 7 \times 3 \\\\
d                       &= 21
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {c} {60}  &= \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
c                &= \dfrac {2 \times 60} {6} \\\\
c                &= \dfrac {2 \times 6 \times 10} {6} \\\\
c                &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {6} \times 10} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
c                &= 2 \times 10 \\\\
c                &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1,6,21,20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the missing numerator $= a,b,c$
<emptyline>
Let the missing denominator $= d$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {3} &= \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac {2 \times 3} {6} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac {6} {6} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac {\cancel {6}} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
a              &= 1
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {b} {18} &= \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {2 \times 18} {6} \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 6} {2 \times 3} \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {3} \times 6} {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
b               &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {7} {d}          &= \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
\dfrac {7 \times 6} {2} &= d \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {7 \times 6} {2} \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {7 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
d                       &= 7 \times 3 \\\\
d                       &= 21
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {c} {60}  &= \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
c                &= \dfrac {2 \times 60} {6} \\\\
c                &= \dfrac {2 \times 6 \times 10} {6} \\\\
c                &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {6} \times 10} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
c                &= 2 \times 10 \\\\
c                &= 20
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1,6,21,20$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

I roll a bouncy ball down the stairs. It bounces once after $2$ steps, then again after double the number of steps. 
It carries on falling, bouncing after triple the number of steps it had bounced originally. 
At this point how many steps lower is the ball than where it started?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of steps before 1st bounce}        &= 2 \\\\
\text{Number of steps before 2nd bounce}        &= 2 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 4 \\\\
\text{Number of steps before 3rd bounce}        &= 2 \times 3 \\\\
                                                &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of steps}        &= 2 + 4 + 6 \\\\
                                    &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

Number of steps the ball lowered after it started rolling $= 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of steps before 1st bounce}        &= 2 \\\\
\text{Number of steps before 2nd bounce}        &= 2 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 4 \\\\
\text{Number of steps before 3rd bounce}        &= 2 \times 3 \\\\
                                                &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of steps}        &= 2 + 4 + 6 \\\\
                                    &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of steps the ball lowered after it started rolling $= 12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Mr Rich decided to share his wealth among his children. 

Richie as the oldest got $\dfrac{1}{3}$, 
Thrifty the second oldest got a $\dfrac{1}{3}$ of what was left, 
Bounty got $\dfrac{1}{2}$ of what was left once Richie and Thrifty got their share. 
The dog, Archie, got the rest. 

If Mr Rich shared $\pounds 9$ million pounds, how much did Archie get?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Archie's share of wealth}     &= \pounds \dfrac {1} {2} \times \left( \dfrac {2} {3} \times \left( \dfrac {2} {3} \times 9000000 \right) \right) \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {2} {3} \times \dfrac {2} {3} \times 9000000 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {1 \times 2 \times 2 \times 9000000} {2 \times 3 \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 3 \times 3 \times 1000000} {2 \times 3 \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 3 \times 1000000} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                    &= 2 \times 1000000 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 2000000
\end{aligned}
$

Share of Archie in Mr Rich wealth $= \pounds 2 \ \text{million}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 2 \ \text{million}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Archie's share of wealth}     &= \pounds \dfrac {1} {2} \times \left( \dfrac {2} {3} \times \left( \dfrac {2} {3} \times 9000000 \right) \right) \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {2} {3} \times \dfrac {2} {3} \times 9000000 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {1 \times 2 \times 2 \times 9000000} {2 \times 3 \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 3 \times 3 \times 1000000} {2 \times 3 \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 3 \times 1000000} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                    &= 2 \times 1000000 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 2000000
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Share of Archie in Mr Rich wealth $= \pounds 2 \ \text{million}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 2 \ \text{million}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $x  \ \spadesuit \  4 = 23$, what number is $x$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
x  \ \spadesuit \  4        &= 23 \\\\
x \times 4 + 3              &= 23 \\\\
x \times 4                  &= 23 - 3 \\\\
x                           &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
x                           &= \dfrac {4 \times 5} {4} \\\\
x                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} \\\\
x                           &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>

x \times 4 + 3              &= 23 \\\\
x \times 4                  &= 23 - 3 \\\\
x                           &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
x                           &= \dfrac {4 \times 5} {4} \\\\
x                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} \\\\
x                           &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

If $y  \ \spadesuit \  y = 39$, what could $y$ be?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
y  \ \spadesuit \  y        &= 39 \\\\
y \times y + 3              &= 39 \\\\
y \times y                  &= 39 - 3 \\\\
y ^ 2                       &= 36 \\\\
y                           &= \sqrt {36} \\\\
y                           &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

y                           &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

Another new mathematical symbol has also been invented.

$\bigstar$ means “multiply together, unless the answer would be odd in which case add together instead”.

So $3  \ \bigstar \  4 = 12$ (because $3 \times 4$ is even)

While $5   \ \bigstar \   7 = 12$ (because $5 \times 7$ would be odd, so we add $5$ and $7$ instead!) 

What is $2   \ \bigstar \   8?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 \ \bigstar \ 8 \\\\
&= 2 \times 8 \\\\
&= 16
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16$

</div>
</div>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 \ \bigstar \ 8 \\\\
&= 2 \times 8 \\\\
&= 16
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $x   \ \bigstar \   2 = 30$, what is the number $x$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
x \ \bigstar \ 2        &= 30 \\\\
x \times 2              &= 30 \\\\
x                       &= \dfrac {30} {2} \\\\
x                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 15} {2} \\\\
x                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 15} {\cancel 2} \\\\
x                       &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>

x                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 15} {\cancel 2} \\\\
x                       &= 15
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $y   \ \bigstar \   3 = 24$, there are two possible values of $y$. What are they?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

Case $1$:

$
\begin{aligned}
y \ \bigstar \ 3        &= 24 \\\\
y \times 3              &= 24 \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {24} {3} \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 8} {3} \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 8} {\cancel 3} \\\\
y                       &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

The first value of y $= 8$

Case $2$:

$
\begin{aligned}
y \ \bigstar \ 3        &= 24 \\\\
y + 3                   &= 24 \\\\
y                       &= 24 - 3 \\\\
y                       &= 21
\end{aligned}
$

The second value of y $= 21$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

The two values of y $= 8, 21$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>

y \times 3              &= 24 \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {24} {3} \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 8} {3} \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 8} {\cancel 3} \\\\
y                       &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The first value of y $= 8$
<emptyline>
Case $2$:
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
y \ \bigstar \ 3        &= 24 \\\\
y + 3                   &= 24 \\\\
y                       &= 24 - 3 \\\\
y                       &= 21
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The second value of y $= 21$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

Chigwell School buys a badge for each student who plays for one of the school teams.
$193$ students receive a badge, and each badge costs $\pounds 6$.

How much does the school have to pay in total?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {School have to pay}      &= \text {No. of students receiving badge} \times \text {Cost of one badge} \\\\
                                &= 193 \times 6 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 1158
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1158$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {School have to pay}      &= \text {No. of students receiving badge} \times \text {Cost of one badge} \\\\
                                &= 193 \times 6 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 1158
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1158$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>chigwell-school--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-1:1:13</p>

</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Four rabbits weigh the same as three cats, and two cats weigh the same as four guinea pigs.
How many rabbits weigh the same as six guinea pigs?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the weight of a rabbit $= r$

Let the weight of a cat $= c$

Let the weight of a guinea pig $= p$

As per the question,

$2c = 4p$

Or,

$1c = 2p$ 

$
\begin {aligned}
4r       &= 3c \\\\
4r       &= 2c + 1c \\\\
4r       &= 4p + 2p \\\\
4r       &= 6p
\end {aligned}
$

Number of rabbits that weigh the same as six guinea pigs $= 4$ rabbits

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the weight of a rabbit $= r$

Let the weight of a cat $= c$

Let the weight of a guinea pig $= p$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
4 r      &= 3 c \\\\
         &= 2 c + 1 c \\\\
         &= 4 p + 2 p \\\\
         &= 6 p
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the weight of a rabbit $= r$

4r       &= 4p + 2p \\\\
4r       &= 6p
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of rabbits that weigh the same as six guinea pigs $= 4$ rabbits
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Olly thinks of a positive whole number. When he divides $60$ by his number, the answer is also a whole number.
How many different numbers could Olly have thought of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Factors of 60}    &= 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 12, 15, 20, 30, 60
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Different numbers Olly thought of}        &= \text{Number of factors of 60} \\\\
                                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Factors of 60}    &= 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 12, 15, 20, 30, 60
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Different numbers Olly thought of}        &= \text{Number of factors of 60} \\\\
                                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the mass of Patrick who has a height of $1.5 \ \text{m}$ and a BMI of $20$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the mass of Patrick (in kg) $= m$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
20                                      &= \dfrac {m} {1.5 ^ 2} \\\\
20 \times 1.5 \times 1.5                &= m \\\\
m                                       &= \dfrac {20 \times 15 \times 15} {10 \times 10} \\\\
m                                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 10 \times 5 \times 3 \times 15} {2 \times 5 \times 10} \\\\
m                                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel 5 \times 3 \times 15} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
m                                       &= 3 \times 15 \\\\
m                                       &= 45 \ \text{kg} 
\end{aligned}
$

Mass of Patrick $= 45 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$45 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the mass of Patrick (in kg) $= m$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
20                                      &= \dfrac {m} {1.5 ^ 2} \\\\
20 \times 1.5 \times 1.5                &= m \\\\
m                                       &= \dfrac {20 \times 15 \times 15} {10 \times 10} \\\\
m                                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 10 \times 5 \times 3 \times 15} {2 \times 5 \times 10} \\\\
m                                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel 5 \times 3 \times 15} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
m                                       &= 3 \times 15 \\\\
m                                       &= 45 \ \text{kg} 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Mass of Patrick $= 45 \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$45 \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the BMI of Bess who is $1.2 \ \text{m}$ tall and has a mass of $36 \ \text{kg}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Body Mass Index (BMI)}            &= \dfrac {36} {1.2 ^ 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {36 \times 10 \times 10} {12 \times 12} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 12 \times 2 \times 5 \times 2 \times 5} {12 \times 2 \times 2 \times 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {12} \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel 2 \times 5} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= 5 \times 5 \\\\
                                        &= 25
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Body Mass Index (BMI)}            &= \dfrac {36} {1.2 ^ 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {36 \times 10 \times 10} {12 \times 12} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 12 \times 2 \times 5 \times 2 \times 5} {12 \times 2 \times 2 \times 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {12} \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel 2 \times 5} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= 5 \times 5 \\\\
                                        &= 25
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$25$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>chigwell-school--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-1:1:31</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>

talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Half of the pupils in a school are boys;
one third of the boys play soccer;
one quarter of the boy soccer players get soccer colours;
one fifth of those who win soccer colours are boys in form 8CC.
There are $10$ boy with soccer in 8CC.

How many pupils are there in the school?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of pupils in the school $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {2} \times \left( \dfrac {1} {3} \times \left( \dfrac {1} {4} \times \left( \dfrac {1} {5} \times a \right) \right) \right)  &= 10 \\\\
\dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {1} {3} \times \dfrac {1} {4} \times \dfrac {1} {5} \times a                                               &= 10 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2 \times 3 \times 4 \times 5}                                                                                               &= 10 \\\\
a                                                                                                                                       &= 10 \times 2 \times 3 \times 4 \times 5 \\\\
a                                                                                                                                       &= 1200
\end{aligned}
$

Total number of pupils in the school $= 1200$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1200$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of pupils in the school $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {2} \times \left( \dfrac {1} {3} \times \left( \dfrac {1} {4} \times \left( \dfrac {1} {5} \times a \right) \right) \right)  &= 10 \\\\
\dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {1} {3} \times \dfrac {1} {4} \times \dfrac {1} {5} \times a                                               &= 10 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2 \times 3 \times 4 \times 5}                                                                                               &= 10 \\\\
a                                                                                                                                       &= 10 \times 2 \times 3 \times 4 \times 5 \\\\
a                                                                                                                                       &= 1200
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total number of pupils in the school $= 1200$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Input} \times 5 + 1  &= 36 \\\\
\text {Input}               &= \dfrac {36 - 1} {5} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {35} {5} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {7 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                            &= 7
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>christs-hospital-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1:1:17</p>

<div class='topics'>

talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Six times a number, minus $4$, is the same as four times the number, plus $10$. What is the number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
6 \times a - 4  &= 4 \times a + 10 \\\\
6a - 4          &= 4a + 10 \\\\
6a - 4a         &= 10 + 4 \\\\
2a              &= 14 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {14} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {7 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a               &= 7
\end {aligned}
$

The number $= 7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
6 \times a - 4  &= 4 \times a + 10 \\\\
6a - 4          &= 4a + 10 \\\\
6a - 4a         &= 10 + 4 \\\\
2a              &= 14 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {14} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {7 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a               &= 7
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>christs-hospital-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1:2:5</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>

<div class='question question'>

**Mobile phone calls**

My monthly mobile phone bill is in two parts: a fixed $\pounds 10$ service charge plus $5 \ \text{pence}$ for each call I make.

In October my bill was $\pounds 21.00$.

In November I made twice as many calls as in October.

Work out the cost of my November bill.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Charge of phone calls}        &= \text{Total bill} - \text{Service charge} \\\\
                                    &= 21 - 10 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 11
\end{aligned}
$

As per question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Phone calls charge in November}        &= 2 \times \text{Phone calls charge in October} \\\\
                                             &= 2 \times 11 \\\\
                                             &= \pounds 22 \\\\
\text{Total of November bill}                &= \text{Service charge} + \text{Phone calls charge} \\\\
                                             &= 10 + 22 \\\\
                                             &= \pounds 32
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 32$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Charge of phone calls}        &= \text{Total bill} - \text{Service charge} \\\\
                                    &= 21 - 10 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 11
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Phone calls charge in November}        &= 2 \times \text{Phone calls charge in October} \\\\
                                             &= 2 \times 11 \\\\
                                             &= \pounds 22 \\\\
\text{Total of November bill}                &= \text{Service charge} + \text{Phone calls charge} \\\\
                                             &= 10 + 22 \\\\
                                             &= \pounds 32
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 32$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


<div class='review'>

<div class='question question'>

**Sweets**

Luke, Fred and Evie have a bag of sweets.

Luke eats half of the sweets.

Fred eats two-thirds of what is left.

Evie eats the remaining $24$ sweets.

Work out how many sweets were in the bag to begin with.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of sweets at the start $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {2} \times \left( \dfrac {1} {3} \times a \right)        &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {1} {3} \times a                       &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2 \times 3}                                             &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {6}                                                      &= 24 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 24 \times 6 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 144
\end{aligned}
$

The number of sweets in the bag at the start $= 144$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$144$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of sweets at the start $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {2} \times \left( \dfrac {1} {3} \times a \right)        &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {1} {3} \times a                       &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2 \times 3}                                             &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {6}                                                      &= 24 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 24 \times 6 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 144
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number of sweets in the bag at the start $= 144$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$144$
</code></pre>
</div>
